Converted from the session transcript (JSONL): `/export` is a user command that Claude Code cannot run itself.
Session time range (UTC): 2026-11-23T08:53:46.945Z → 2026-11-23T17:03:04.035Z.

How to read this log:
- It contains the user messages, Claude's replies and tool calls with **shortened** inputs and outputs.
- Claude's internal thinking and the harness's `<system-reminder>` context blocks are omitted.
- Attached task files are shortened to a preview; the full text is in `docs/tasks/`.
- Automated notifications (background tasks) and subagent hand-backs are labelled "System / agent message": they are not typed by the user.
- Secrets (connection strings, `npg_` tokens, Neon hosts, `.env` values), e-mail addresses (except reserved test domains such as `example.com`), the Windows user name and the Vercel team name are replaced with `[REDACTED]` / `[USER]`.
- It covers the whole session, including the review round after the owner's answers, up to the final export (the last export step itself is not included).

# Task 15 – Homepage built from backend blocks (+ footer "other projects", README "About the author")

**Model:** Opus · **Effort:** high
**Start only after Task 16a is merged** (its migration is `00005`; this task's migration must be the next number). Runs in parallel with Task 16b → use a worktree:
ʼʼʼ
git worktree add ../ENEBA-homework-15 -b task/15-homepage origin/main
ʼʼʼ
This PR has a **migration** → owner runs *Migrate production* from this branch before merging. Remove the worktree after the PR is opened.

## Goal
The assignment: *"The landing page is assembled from several different blocks, such as a banner, best sellers or deals. Each block gets its content from its own backend service or database, so the frontend only displays what it receives and nothing is hardcoded there."* Build it in Eneba's layout (compare with eneba.com's homepage at 1440/390, CLAUDE.md rule).

## Decisions (made by the owner – do not change)
- **H5 Block registry in the DB:** table `homepage_blocks` (`key`, `type`, `title`, `position`, `enabled`, `config jsonb` – e.g. sort/limit/"view all" link for product rows). `GET /api/homepage` returns the enabled blocks in order: `{ blocks: [ { key, type, title, endpoint } ] }`. The frontend has **one renderer per block type** and fetches each block from its own `endpoint` – it contains no block content, order or titles.
- **Each block has its own endpoint and data source** (logically separate services; README explains they could be split into real services):
  - **H1 hero** `GET /api/blocks/hero` – slides from table `homepage_slides` (`position`, `enabled`, `title`, `subtitle`, `badge`, `image_url`, `image_url_mobile`, optional `product_slug`, optional `cta_label` + `cta_href`). For a slide with `product_slug`, the endpoint adds the **live** cheapest price / in-stock flag from the catalog and the CTA goes to the product page.
    - Slide 1: **Innert** (a friend's game in development, used with the author's permission): title "INNERT", subtitle "Realms · Raids · Dragons", badge "Coming soon", **no button** (its repository is private – no dead links). Images: the owner's files in `C:\PROJEKTAI\ENEBA\innert\` – desktop `hero-3840x1240.png`, phone `key-art-clean-1920x1080.png` (fallback option `01-golden-hour-lake-3840x2160.png` if the hero is too light behind the text). // Package server holds the HTTP router and handlers. It is shared by the
// Vercel function (api/index.go) and the local dev server (cmd/local).
package server

import "net/http"

// Deps are the things handlers need from the outside world. They are passed
// in explicitly (no globals inside handlers), which keeps handlers testable.
type Deps struct {
	DB      DBPinger
	Catalog CatalogReader
	Auth    AuthStore
	Orders  OrderStore
}

// NewRouter builds the application router using only the standard library.
// Go 1.22+ ServeMux supports "METHOD /path" patterns and {id} wildcards.
//
// The whole mux is wrapped in requireSameOrigin (origin.go): every POST, PUT,
// PATCH and DELETE must come from our own site (CSRF protection), including
// the endpoints that later tasks add.
func NewRouter(deps Deps) http.Handler {
	mux := http.NewServeMux()

	mux.HandleFunc("GET /api/health", healthHandler(deps.DB))
	// Same path, any other method -> JSON 405 (the more specific GET pattern wins).
	mux.HandleFunc("/api/health", methodNotAllowed)

	// Catalog: public and cacheable. These handlers never read the session
	// cookie, so the cached answer is the same for every visitor.
	mux.HandleFunc("GET /api/products", productsHandler(deps.Catalog))
	mux.HandleFunc("/api/products", methodNotAllowed)
	mux.HandleFunc("GET /api/products/{slug}", productHandler(deps.Catalog))
	mux.HandleFunc("/api/products/{slug}", methodNotAllowed)
	mux.HandleFunc("GET /api/search/suggest", suggestHandler(deps.Cat
… [truncated; original 10137 chars]
```

package catalog

import "errors"

// ErrNotFound is returned by GetProduct when no product has the given slug.
var ErrNotFound = errors.New("catalog: product not found")

// Sort is one of the allowed listing orders. The SQL for each one is a fixed
// line in store.go (orderBy): user text never reaches ORDER BY.
type Sort string

const (
	SortPopularity Sort = "popularity" // default without a search
	SortRelevance  Sort = "relevance"  // "Best match": default WITH a search; without one = popularity
	SortPriceAsc   Sort = "price_asc"
	SortPriceDesc  Sort = "price_desc"
	SortDiscount   Sort = "discount"
	SortNewest     Sort = "newest"
	SortTitle      Sort = "title"
)

// Sorts lists every valid Sort (used for validation and error messages).
var Sorts = []Sort{SortPopularity, SortRelevance, SortPriceAsc, SortPriceDesc, SortDiscount, SortNewest, SortTitle}

// Paging limits (decided by the owner).
const (
	DefaultPageSize = 24
	MaxPageSize     = 48
)

// Filter is a validated listing request. Empty Platforms/Regions = no filter.
// Prices are integer cents; nil = no bound. The HTTP layer validates the
// values (see pkg/server) - the store trusts them except for Sort.
type Filter struct {
	Query     string   // search text, already normalized (NormalizeQuery); "" = no search
	Platforms []string // platform slugs, any of them matches
	Regions   []string // region slugs, any of them matches
	MinPrice  *int64
	MaxPrice  *int64
	Sort      Sort
	Page      int // 1-based
	PageSize  in

// Cache-Control values (decided by the owner). The Vercel CDN may serve a
// stored copy for s-maxage seconds without calling the function, and for the
// following stale-while-revalidate seconds it serves the old copy while it
// fetches a fresh one in the background.
//
// Short staleness is safe because the catalog is only for BROWSING: checkout
// (a later task) re-checks stock and price in the database before it takes
// money, so a 60 s old price on a card can never cause a wrong charge.
const (
	cacheList    = "public, s-maxage=60, stale-while-revalidate=300"
	cacheProduct = "public, s-maxage=15, stale-while-revalidate=60"
	// Suggestions change only when the catalog does (titles/aliases); the price
	// shown in the dropdown may be 5 minutes old - the product page shows the live one.
	cacheSuggest = "public, s-maxage=300, stale-while-revalidate=600"
)

// requestTimeout bounds one request's database work: Neon cold start and the
// single connect retry included (the arithmetic is in pkg/db/retry.go).
const requestTimeout = db.RequestTimeout

// CatalogReader is everything the catalog handlers need from the data layer.
// Like DBPinger, it is a small interface: handlers do not know about pgx, and
// tests plug in a fake that returns canned data or errors.

import { useQuery } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { useId } from "react";
import { productListQuery } from "../../api/queries.ts";
import { ProductGrid, ProductGridSkeleton } from "./ProductGrid.tsx";
import styles from "./PopularProducts.module.css";

/** How many products the "Popular right now" block shows (owner decision Q5). */
export const POPULAR_COUNT = 8;

/**
 * "Popular right now": the first 8 products of the default listing (most popular first), shown under
 * "No results for 'q'" so a search that found nothing still offers somewhere to go (owner decision Q5).
 * It is an extra: if it fails to load it simply is not shown (the empty-state message above already explains the page).
 */
export function PopularProducts() {
  const headingId = useId();
  const query = useQuery(productListQuery({ pageSize: POPULAR_COUNT }));

  if (query.isError) return null;

  return (
    <section className={styles.section} aria-labelledby={headingId}>
      <h2 id={headingId} className={styles.title}>
        Popular right now
      </h2>
      {query.data ? <ProductGrid items={query.data.items} /> : <ProductGridSkeleton count={POPULAR_COUNT} />}
    </section>
  );
}
/* "Popular right now" under a search without results (DESIGN.md 3.2). */
.section {
  margin-top: var(--space-5);
}

/* Like the section headings on the product page: 18px bold. */
.title {
  margin-bottom: var(--space-4);
  font-size: var(--font-size-section);

/** An external link: opens in a new tab and tells screen-reader users so. */
function ExternalLink({ href, children, className }: { href: string; children: string; className?: string }) {
  return (
    <a href={href} className={className} target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">
      {children}
      <VisuallyHidden> (opens in a new tab)</VisuallyHidden>

/*
 * Design tokens (see docs/DESIGN.md for the source of every value and the usage rules).
 * Components must use these variables - never hardcode colors, sizes or shadows.
 * Dark theme only: Eneba is a deep-purple "dark" design, so there is no light variant.
 */
:root {
  color-scheme: dark; /* native controls and scrollbars follow the dark theme */

  /* ---------- Colors: backgrounds and surfaces ---------- */
  --color-bg: #4618ac; /* page background (body) */
  --color-bg-deep: #351183; /* nav strip, popups, drawers header: darker than the page */
  --color-surface: #5825cc; /* lighter panels: filter sidebar, sections inside drawers */
  --color-surface-card: #1f0a4d; /* product card body (darkest surface) */
  --color-surface-row: rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.3); /* offer row on the product page */
  --color-surface-inset: rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.2); /* text input / select background */
  --color-surface-inset-hover: rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.35);
  --color-surface-inset-focus: rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.3);
  --color-surface-hover: rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.1); /* hover on menu items / list rows (derived) */
  --color-overlay: rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.55); /* dim layer / label over cover images */
  --color-footer-bg: rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.7);

	// IGDB allows 4 requests per second. We make only a few, but still keep at

	minGap = 300 * time.Millisecond
)

// Client talks to Twitch (for the token) and to IGDB. It is used only by the
// local cmd/igdb-snapshot tool.
//
// SECURITY: the client id, the secret and the token are never put into a URL
// (Go's HTTP errors print the URL) and never into an error message.
type Client struct {
	HTTP     *http.Client
	clientID string
	token    string
	last     time.Time // time of the previous request (rate limit)
}

// NewClient returns a client with a sane timeout.
func NewClient(clientID string) *Client {
	return &Client{HTTP: &http.Client{Timeout: 30 * time.Second}, clientID: clientID}
}

// Authenticate gets an app access token with the OAuth 2.0 **client credentials**

## Claude · 2026-11-23T08:56:56.833Z

I'll fetch the artwork ids once with a scratchpad script that reads the credentials from `.env` itself and never prints them.

**Tool: Write**

```
C:\Users\[USER]\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\27133936c4ab5668\60d9fad0-2c1e-4660-a781-65a98ec415b1\scratchpad\igdb_artworks.py
---
# One-off: list IGDB artworks (wide key art) for the hero slides. Credentials are read from the repo's .env
# and never printed. Output: image ids + sizes only.
import json
import sys
import urllib.parse
import urllib.request

env_path, out_dir = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
env = {}
for line in open(env_path, encoding="utf-8"):
    line = line.strip()
    if line and not line.startswith("#") and "=" in line:
        k, v = line.split("=", 1)
        env[k.strip()] = v.strip().strip('"').strip("'")

cid, secret = env["IGDB_CLIENT_ID"], env["IGDB_CLIENT_SECRET"]
body = urllib.parse.urlencode({"client_id": cid, "client_secret": secret, "grant_type": "client_credentials"}).encode()
tok = json.load(urllib.request.urlopen(urllib.request.Request("https://id.twitch.tv/oauth2/token", data=body)))["access_token"]


def query(endpoint, q):
    req = urllib.request.Request("https://api.igdb.com/v4/" + endpoint, data=q.encode(),
                                 headers={"Client-ID": cid, "Authorization": "Bearer " + tok, "Accept": "application/json"})
    return json.load(urllib.request.urlopen(req))


arts = query("artworks", "fields game,image_id,width,height; where game = (1020,1942); limit 50;")
for a in arts:
    print("artwork", a.get("game"), a.get("image_id"), a.get("width"), a.get("height"))
    url = "https://images.igdb.com/igdb/image/upload/t_screenshot_med/%s.jpg" % a["image_id"]
    urllib.request.urlretrieve(url, "%s/%s_%s.jpg" % (out_dir, a.get("game"), a["image_id"]))
```

// Measure parts of eneba.com's home page by probing points (computed styles + rectangles).
import puppeteer from "puppeteer-core";
const browser = await puppeteer.launch({
  executablePath: "C:/Program Files (x86)/Microsoft/Edge/Application/msedge.exe",
  headless: "new",
});
const probes = {
  desktop: [[500, 300], [1080, 300], [1225, 300], [1370, 300], [180, 500], [180, 535], [700, 820], [700, 1200], [700, 1150], [700, 1700]],
  mobile: [[50, 150], [150, 150], [70, 295], [70, 326], [195, 1355], [195, 1470], [195, 1430]],
};
for (const [w, h, name] of [[1440, 900, "desktop"], [390, 844, "mobile"]]) {
  const page = await browser.newPage();
  await page.setViewport({ width: w, height: h, isMobile: w < 600, hasTouch: w < 600 });
  await page.goto("https://www.eneba.com/", { waitUntil: "networkidle2", timeout: 60000 });
  await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 2000));
  const res = await page.evaluate(async (pts) => {
    const out = [];
    for (const [x, y] of pts) {
      window.scrollTo(0, Math.max(0, y - 300));
      await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 400));
      const els = document.elementsFromPoint(x, y - scrollY).slice(0, 6);
      out.push({
        at: [x, y],
        chain: els.map((el) => {
          const b = el.getBoundingClientRect();
          const cs = getComputedStyle(el);
          return {
            tag: el.tagName.toLowerCase(),
            text: (el.innerText || el.alt || "").trim().slice(0, 30),
            rect: [Math.round(b.x), Math.round(b.y + scrollY), Math.round(b.width), Math.round(b.height)],
            bg: cs.backgroundColor !== "rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)" ? cs.backgroundColor : undefined,
            bgi: cs.backgroundImage !== "none" ? cs.backgroundImage.slice(0, 60) : undefined,
            fs: cs.fontSize, fw: cs.fontWeight, pad: cs.padding, radius: cs.borderRadius !== "0px" ? cs.borderRadius : undefined,
            gap: cs.gap !== "normal" ? cs.gap : undefined, ofx: cs.overflowX !== "visible" ? cs.overflowX : undefined,
          };
        }),
      });
    }
    return out;
  }, probes[name]);
  console.log("=====", name);
  for (const p of res) {
    console.log("@", p.at.join(","));
    for (const c of p.chain) console.log("   ", JSON.stringify(c));
  }
  await page.close();
}
await browser.close();

func Counts(ctx context.Context, tx pgx.Tx) ([]TableCount, error) {
	var out []TableCount
	for _, t := range CatalogTables {
		var n int64
		// t comes from the fixed list above, never from user input.
		if err := tx.QueryRow(ctx, "SELECT count(*) FROM "+t).Scan(&n); err != nil {
			return nil, err
		}
		out = append(out, TableCount{t, n})
	}
	return out, nil
}

// Reset deletes all catalog rows (children first) and restarts every identity at 1,
// so the same snapshot always gets the same ids. Both steps are transactional in
// Postgres: if the seed fails later, the ROLLBACK brings the old data AND the old
// identity counters back. (setval() would NOT be rolled back, so it is not used.)
func Reset(ctx context.Context, tx pgx.Tx) error {
	if err := refuseWhenOrdersExist(ctx, tx); err != nil {
		return err
	}
	for _, t := range CatalogTables {
		if _, err := tx.Exec(ctx, "DELETE FROM "+t); err != nil {
			return fmt.Errorf("delete from %s: %w", t, err)
		}
		if _, err := tx.Exec(ctx, "ALTER TABLE "+t+" ALTER COLUMN id RESTART WITH 1"); err != nil {
			return fmt.Errorf("restart identity of %s: %w", t, err)
		}
	}
	return nil
}
	}
	return migratedPool
}

// refuses a Neon host.

---
-- +goose Up
-- Home page (Task 15): the block registry and the content of the hero and the side tiles.
-- Design and reasons: docs/DATA_MODEL.md ("Home page tables").
--
-- Backward compatible (expand/contract): three NEW tables, nothing else changes.
-- The code that is live while this runs never reads them.
--
-- The CONTENT rows are inserted here, not by cmd/seed: the catalog seed (and its golden checksum)
-- stays as it is, and production needs no reseed (`seed --reset` refuses once orders exist).
-- No foreign key points into the catalog (product_slug is plain text): each block is a logically
-- separate service, and the catalog reseed deletes and re-inserts products. The API skips a slide
-- whose product does not exist or is out of stock.

-- The registry: which blocks the home page shows, in which order. GET /api/homepage returns the
-- enabled rows ordered by position; the frontend only knows how to DRAW each type.
CREATE TABLE homepage_blocks (
    id       bigint  GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY PRIMARY KEY,
    -- Stable name, used in the URL of a product row: /api/blocks/products/{key}.
    key      text    NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    type     text    NOT NULL,
    title    text    NOT NULL,
    position integer NOT NULL,
    enabled  boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true,
    -- Per-type settings, e.g. a product row's {"sort": "discount", "limit": 5, "viewAllHref": "/store?sort=discount"}.
    -- The Go code validates it (pkg/homepage/config.go); a bad config is a 500, never a silent default.
    config   jsonb   NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}',
    CONSTRAINT homepage_blocks_key_format CHECK (key ~ '^[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*$'),
    -- A new type needs new code (an endpoint and a renderer), so it also needs a migration.
    CONSTRAINT homepage_blocks_type_valid CHECK (type IN ('hero', 'tiles', 'platforms', 'product_row')),
    CONSTRAINT homepage_blocks_title_not_blank CHECK (btrim(title) <> ''),
    CONSTRAINT homepage_blocks_config_object CHECK (jsonb_typeof(config) = 'object')
);
-- At most ONE hero, one tiles and one platforms block: their endpoints (/api/blocks/hero ...) have no key.
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX homepage_blocks_one_per_single_type ON homepage_blocks (type) WHERE type <> 'product_row';

-- The hero carousel's slides. A slide either links nowhere (no button), to a product
-- (product_slug: the API adds the live price and links to /product/{slug}) or to a page (cta_href).
CREATE TABLE homepage

package orders

import (
	"context"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"time"

	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5"
	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgconn"
	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgxpool"

	"github.com/dovydas472/ENEBA-homework/pkg/db"
)

// ConnSource hands the Store a database connection (same contract as in pkg/catalog and
// pkg/auth): *db.Lazy implements it, with the one cold-start retry.
type ConnSource interface {
	Acquire(ctx context.Context) (*pgxpool.Conn, error)
}

type fixedPool struct{ p *pgxpool.Pool }

func (f fixedPool) Acquire(ctx context.Context) (*pgxpool.Conn, error) { return db.Acquire(ctx, f.p) }

// Store runs the checkout transactions. All SQL is constant text; user input only travels as
// query parameters. `now` is the clock: tests replace it (WithClock) to simulate the 15 minutes
// without sleeping. Every time comparison in this file uses it - never the database's now() -
// so a test that moves the clock moves ALL of them together.
type Store struct {
	src ConnSource
	now func() time.Time
}

// NewStore builds a Store on a (lazy) connection source, with the real clock.
func NewStore(src ConnSource) *Store { return &Store{src: src, now: time.Now} }

// NewStoreFromPool builds a Store on a ready pool (tests).
func NewStoreFromPool(p *pgxpool.Pool) *Store { return &Store{src: fixedPool{p}, now: time.Now} }

// WithClock returns a copy of the store that reads the time from now (tests).
func (s *Store) WithClock(now func() time.Time) *Store { return &Store{src: s.src, now: now} }

---
// Package homepage serves the home page blocks (Task 15). The page is assembled from a REGISTRY
// of blocks in the database (homepage_blocks); every block has its own endpoint and data source:
//
//	hero         GET /api/blocks/hero              homepage_slides + live prices from the catalog
//	tiles        GET /api/blocks/tiles             homepage_tiles + cover images from the catalog
//	platforms    GET /api/blocks/platforms         platform counts from the catalog
//	product_row  GET /api/blocks/products/{key}    the catalog listing, configured by the block row
//
// The frontend only knows how to DRAW each block type; which blocks exist, their order, titles and
// content all come from here. The blocks are logically separate services: they share this package
// and one database today, but each could move to its own service without the frontend noticing.
package homepage

import (
	"errors"

	"github.com/dovydas472/ENEBA-homework/pkg/catalog"
)

// ErrNotFound: the block does not exist or is disabled (the API answers 404 for both).
var ErrNotFound = errors.New("homepage: block not found")

// Block types. The database allows exactly these (CHECK homepage_blocks_type_valid).
const (
	TypeHero       = "hero"
	TypeTiles      = "tiles"
	TypePlatforms  = "platforms"
	TypeProductRow = "product_row"
)

// Block is one row of homepage_blocks. Config is the raw JSON object; it is decoded per type
// (config.go), because each type has different settings.
type Block struct {
	Key    string
	Type   string
	Title  string
	Config []byte
}

// Slide is one row of homepage_slides (nil = NULL in the database).
type Slide struct {
	ID                int64
	Title             string
	Subtitle          *string
	Badge             *string
	ImageURL          string
	ImageSrcset       *string
	ImageURLMobile    string
	ImageSrcsetMobile *string
	ProductSlug       *string // set = a product slide: live price, the button goes to its product page
	CTALabel          *string
	CTAHref           *string
}

// Tile is one row of homepage_tiles.
type Tile struct {
	ID       int64
	Title    string
	Subtitle *string
	Href     string
	Accent   string
	Covers   []byte // raw JSON object or nil (no collage)
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
// JSON responses. Lists are never nil, so the JSON has [] and not null.
// --------------------------------------------------

---
package homepage

import (
	"bytes"
	"encoding/json"
	"fmt"
	"regexp"
	"slices"

	"github.com/dovydas472/ENEBA-homework/pkg/catalog"
)

// The JSON settings in the database are checked here, strictly: an unknown field, a wrong type or a
// value out of range is an ERROR (the API answers 500 and logs it), never a silent default. A typo in
// a config must be noticed, not quietly show the wrong products.

// MaxRowItems is the most cards a product row may ask for.
const MaxRowItems = 12

// rowSorts are the sorts a product row may use: every Store sort except "relevance", which only
// means something together with a search text.
var rowSorts = []catalog.Sort{
	catalog.SortPopularity, catalog.SortDiscount, catalog.SortNewest,
	catalog.SortPriceAsc, catalog.SortPriceDesc, catalog.SortTitle,
}

// internalHref: a path on our own site ("/store?sort=discount"), not "//other.site" or "javascript:".
// The same rule as the CHECK constraints of the tables.
var internalHref = regexp.MustCompile(`^/([^/]|$)`)

// iconName: a lucide-style icon name ("gamepad-2"). The frontend maps names it knows to icons.
var iconName = regexp.MustCompile(`^[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*$`)

// decodeStrict decodes a JSON object and rejects unknown fields and trailing data.
func decodeStrict(raw []byte, out any) error {
	dec := json.NewDecoder(bytes.NewReader(raw))
	dec.DisallowUnknownFields()
	if err := dec.Decode(out); err != nil {
		return err
	}
	if dec.More() {
		return fmt.Errorf("unexpected data after the JSON object")

// ParseProductRowConfig decodes and validates a product row's config.
func ParseProductRowConfig(raw []byte) (ProductRowConfig, error) {

	}

	}

		return ProductRowConfig{}, fmt.Errorf("product row config: limit %d is not 1-%d", c.Limit, MaxRowItems)

package homepage

import (
	"context"
	"errors"
	"fmt"

	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5"
	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgxpool"

	"github.com/dovydas472/ENEBA-homework/pkg/db"
)

// ConnSource hands the Store a database connection (same contract as pkg/catalog and pkg/orders):
// *db.Lazy implements it, with the one cold-start retry.
type ConnSource interface {
	Acquire(ctx context.Context) (*pgxpool.Conn, error)
}

type fixedPool struct{ p *pgxpool.Pool }

func (f fixedPool) Acquire(ctx context.Context) (*pgxpool.Conn, error) { return db.Acquire(ctx, f.p) }

// Store reads the home page's own tables. Only ENABLED rows ever leave it: a disabled block, slide
// or tile is invisible to the rest of the code. Ties in position are broken by id, so the order is stable.
type Store struct{ src ConnSource }

// NewStore builds a Store on a (lazy) connection source.
func NewStore(src ConnSource) *Store { return &Store{src: src} }

// NewStoreFromPool builds a Store on a ready pool (tests).
func NewStoreFromPool(p *pgxpool.Pool) *Store { return &Store{src: fixedPool{p}} }

const blockColumns = `key, type, title, config`

const blocksSQL = `SELECT ` + blockColumns + ` FROM homepage_blocks WHERE enabled ORDER BY position, id`

const blockByKeySQL = `SELECT ` + blockColumns + ` FROM homepage_blocks WHERE enabled AND key = $1`

// A unique index allows only one block of each type except product_row, so this is at most one row.
const blockByTypeSQL = `SELECT ` + blockColumns + ` FROM homepage_blocks WHERE enabled AND type = $1`

const slidesSQL = `
SELECT id, title, subtitle, badge, image_url, image_srcset, image_url_mobile, image_srcset_mobile,
       product_slug, cta_label, cta_href
FROM homepage_slides
WHERE enabled
ORDER BY position, id`

const tilesSQL = `
SELECT id, title, subtitle, href, accent, covers
FROM homepage_tiles
WHERE enabled
ORDER BY position, id`

// Blocks returns the enabled blocks in page order.
func (s *Store) Blocks(ctx context.Context) ([]Block, error) {
	conn, err := s.src.Acquire(ctx)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	defer conn.Release()

	rows, err := conn.Query(ctx, blocksSQL)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("homepage: blocks: %w", err)
	}
	out, err := pgx.CollectRows(rows, scanBlock)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("homepage: blocks: %w", err)
	}
	if out == nil {
		out = []Block{}
	}
	return out, nil
}

package homepage

import (
	"context"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"log"

	"github.com/dovydas472/ENEBA-homework/pkg/catalog"
)

// Content is the home page's own data. *Store implements it; unit tests use a fake.
type Content interface {
	Blocks(ctx context.Context) ([]Block, error)
	BlockByKey(ctx context.Context, key string) (Block, error)
	BlockByType(ctx context.Context, blockType string) (Block, error)
	Slides(ctx context.Context) ([]Slide, error)
	Tiles(ctx context.Context) ([]Tile, error)
}

// Catalog is the part of the catalog the blocks reuse: the Store listing (product rows, tile covers,
// platform counts) and the product page (live price of a hero slide). *catalog.Store implements it.
type Catalog interface {
	ListProducts(ctx context.Context, f catalog.Filter) (catalog.Page, error)
	GetProduct(ctx context.Context, slug string) (catalog.ProductDetail, error)
}

// Service builds the response of each block endpoint from the home page tables and the catalog.
// Every method is one block's "service"; they share nothing but the two data sources.
type Service struct {
	content Content
	catalog Catalog
}

// NewService builds a Service.
func NewService(content Content, cat Catalog) *Service {
	return &Service{content: content, catalog: cat}
}

// coversPerTile is how many cover images a tile's collage shows.
const coversPerTile = 3

// Registry is GET /api/homepage: the enabled blocks in order, each with the endpoint of its content.
func (s *Service) Registry(ctx context.Context) (Registry, error) {
	blocks, err := s.content.Blocks(ctx)
	if err != nil {
		return Registry{}, err
	}
	out := Registry{Blocks: []RegistryEntry{}}
	for _, b := range blocks {
		endpoint, ok := Endpoint(b)
		if !ok {
			// Cannot happen while the CHECK constraint lists the same types; skip rather than break the page.
			log.Printf("homepage: block %q has an unknown type %q - skipped", b.Key, b.Type)
			continue
		}
		out.Blocks = append(out.Blocks, RegistryEntry{Key: b.Key, Type: b.Type, Title: b.Title, Endpoint: endpoint})
	}
	return out, nil
}

---
package server

import (
	"context"
	"errors"
	"net/http"

	"github.com/dovydas472/ENEBA-homework/pkg/homepage"
)

// cacheHomepage: the registry and every block (owner decision, Task 15). Home page content changes
// rarely, so the CDN may keep an answer for 5 minutes and then serve it for 10 more while it fetches a
// fresh one. Prices on the page may therefore be a few minutes old - checkout re-prices in the database.
// Consequence: in production, turning a block on or off is visible within about 5 minutes, without a deploy.
const cacheHomepage = "public, s-maxage=300, stale-while-revalidate=600"

// HomepageReader is everything the home page handlers need. *homepage.Service implements it;
// tests use a fake. Like CatalogReader it hides the database from the HTTP layer.
type HomepageReader interface {
	Registry(ctx context.Context) (homepage.Registry, error)
	Hero(ctx context.Context) (homepage.Hero, error)
	Tiles(ctx context.Context) (homepage.Tiles, error)
	Platforms(ctx context.Context) (homepage.Platforms, error)
	ProductRow(ctx context.Context, key string) (homepage.ProductRow, error)
}

// homepageHandler turns one HomepageReader method into an HTTP handler with the shared rules:
//   - block does not exist or is disabled -> 404 (never cached)
//   - DB unreachable -> 503 generic; any other error (e.g. a bad config in the table) -> 500 generic
//   - success -> 200 JSON, cacheable for 5 minutes
//
// `load` gets the request (a product row needs its {key}) and returns the value to send as JSON.
func homepageHandler(h HomepageReader, what string, load func(ctx context.Context, h HomepageReader, r *http.Request) (any, error)) http.HandlerFunc {
	return func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		if h == nil {
			unavailable(w, what, errors.New("no homepage service configured"))
			return
		}
		ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(r.Context(), requestTimeout)
		defer cancel()
		v, err := load(ctx, h, r)
		if errors.Is(err, homepage.ErrNotFound) {
			writeError(w, http.StatusNotFound, "block not found")
			return
		}
		if err != nil {
			storeFailed(w, what, err)
			return
		}
		writeCached(w, http.StatusOK, cacheHomepage, v)
	}
}

func loadRegistry(ctx context.Context, h HomepageReader, _ *http.Request) (any, error) {
	return h.Registry(ctx)
}

func loadHero(ctx context.Context, h HomepageReader, _ *http.Request) (any, error) {
	return h.Hero(ctx)
}

func TestParseProductRowConfig_valid(t *testing.T) {
	c, err := ParseProductRowConfig([]byte(`{"sort": "discount", "limit": 5, "viewAllHref": "/store?sort=discount"}`))
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("unexpected error: %v", err)
	}
	want := ProductRowConfig{Sort: catalog.SortDiscount, Limit: 5, ViewAllHref: "/store?sort=discount"}
	if c != want {
		t.Errorf("got %+v, want %+v", c, want)
	}
}

// A config mistake must be an error (500), never a silent default.
func TestParseProductRowConfig_rejectsBadValues(t *testing.T) {
	cases := map[string]string{
		"unknown field":         `{"sort": "discount", "limit": 5, "viewAllHref": "/store", "colour": "red"}`,
		"typo in a field name":  `{"sort": "discount", "limt": 5, "viewAllHref": "/store"}`,

		"limit 0":               `{"sort": "newest", "limit": 0, "viewAllHref": "/store"}`,
		"limit too big":         `{"sort": "newest", "limit": 13, "viewAllHref": "/store"}`,
		"external link":         `{"sort": "newest", "limit": 5, "viewAllHref": "https://example.com"}`,
		"protocol-relative":     `{"sort": "newest", "limit": 5, "viewAllHref": "//example.com"}`,
		"missing link":          `{"sort": "newest", "limit": 5}`,
		"wrong type":            `{"sort": "newest", "limit": "5", "viewAllHref": "/store"}`,
		"two objects":           `{"sort": "newest", "limit": 5, "viewAllHref": "/store"} {}`,
		"not an object at all":  `[1, 2]`,

				t.Errorf("expected an error for %s", raw)
			}
		})
	}
}

package homepage

import (

// fakeContent stands in for the Store. It holds only ENABLED rows, like the real Store returns:
// a disabled block is simply not in `blocks`.
type fakeContent struct {
	blocks []Block
	slides []Slide
	tiles  []Tile
	err    error
}

func (f *fakeContent) Blocks(context.Context) ([]Block, error) { return f.blocks, f.err }

func (f *fakeContent) BlockByKey(_ context.Context, key string) (Block, error) {
	if f.err != nil {
		return Block{}, f.err
	}
	for _, b := range f.blocks {
		if b.Key == key {
			return b, nil
		}
	}
	return Block{}, ErrNotFound
}

func (f *fakeContent) BlockByType(_ context.Context, blockType string) (Block, error) {
	if f.err != nil {
		return Block{}, f.err
	}
	for _, b := range f.blocks {
		if b.Type == blockType {
			return b, nil
		}
	}
	return Block{}, ErrNotFound
}

func (f *fakeContent) Slides(context.Context) ([]Slide, error) { return f.slides, f.err }
func (f *fakeContent) Tiles(context.Context) ([]Tile, error)   { return f.tiles, f.err }

// fakeCatalog returns canned products and remembers the filters it was asked for.
type fakeCatalog struct {
	products map[string]catalog.ProductDetail
	page     catalog.Page
	filters  []catalog.Filter
	err      error
}

func (f *fakeCatalog) ListProducts(_ context.Context, flt catalog.Filter) (catalog.Page, error) {
	f.filters = append(f.filters, flt)
	return f.page, f.err
}

func (f *fakeCatalog) GetProduct(_ context.Context, slug string) (catalog.ProductDetail, error) {
	if f.err != nil {
		return catalog.ProductDetail{}, f.err
	}
	d, ok := f.products[slug]
	if !ok {
		return catalog.ProductDetail{}, catalog.ErrNotFound
	}
	return d, nil
}

<details><summary>Result</summary>

```
188:// Errors that mean "the database cannot be reached": infrastructure -> 503.
196:		"network error":                  &net.OpError{Op: "read", Err: errors.New("connection reset " + secret)},
230:	assertGenericError(t, rec, http.StatusServiceUnavailable, "service temporarily unavailable")
//
// A query that merely ran out of time (context.DeadlineExceeded) is NOT
// "unavailable": the database answered, it was just slow. Only the connect
// phase counts as unreachable, and that is already wrapped in ErrUnavailable
// by Acquire.
func IsUnavailable(err error) bool {
	if err == nil {
		return false
	}
	if errors.Is(err, ErrUnavailable) || errors.Is(err, ErrNotConfigured) ||
		errors.Is(err, ErrBadConfig) || errors.Is(err, ErrPoolCreate) {
		return true
	}
	if errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded) || errors.Is(err, context.Canceled) {
		return false
	}
	var pgErr *pgconn.PgError
	if errors.As(err, &pgErr) {
		return transientPgCode(pgErr.Code)
	}
	var connErr *pgconn.ConnectError
	if errors.As(err, &connErr) {
		return true
	}
	var netErr net.Error
	if errors.As(err, &netErr) {
		return true
	}
	return errors.Is(err, pgconn.ErrConnClosed)
}
```

package homepage_test

// Integration tests: the real router + homepage.Service + homepage.Store + catalog.Store against a
// real Postgres with every migration applied (00006 inserts the home page content) and the
// deterministic demo catalog (pkg/testdb). Skipped when TEST_DATABASE_URL is empty; CI runs them.
//
// Some tests switch a block off and on again. They restore the row in t.Cleanup, and the tests of
// this package run one after another (none calls t.Parallel), so they never see each other's changes.

import (
	"context"
	"encoding/json"
	"net/http"
	"net/http/httptest"
	"reflect"
	"testing"

	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5"

)

type env struct {
	pool    *pgxpool.Pool
	catalog *catalog.Store
	router  http.Handler
}

func newEnv(t *testing.T) env {
	t.Helper()
	pool := testdb.Pool(t)
	cat := catalog.NewStoreFromPool(pool)
	svc := homepage.NewService(homepage.NewStoreFromPool(pool), cat)
	return env{pool: pool, catalog: cat, router: server.NewRouter(server.Deps{Catalog: cat, Homepage: svc})}
}

// get calls the router and decodes the JSON body into out. It returns the status code.
func (e env) get(t *testing.T, path string, out any) int {
	t.Helper()
	rec := httptest.NewRecorder()
	e.router.ServeHTTP(rec, httptest.NewRequest(http.MethodGet, path, nil))
	if rec.Code == http.StatusOK && out != nil {
		if err := json.Unmarshal(rec.Body.Bytes(), out); err != nil {
			t.Fatalf("GET %s: body is not JSON: %v", path, err)
		}
	}
	return rec.Code
}

// setEnabled switches a block on or off and puts the old value back when the test ends.
func (e env) setEnabled(t *testing.T, key string, enabled bool) {
	t.Helper()
	ctx := context.Background()
	var old bool
	if err := e.pool.QueryRow(ctx, `SELECT enabled FROM homepage_blocks WHERE key = $1`, key).Scan(&old); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("read block %q: %v", key, err)
	}
	t.Cleanup(func() {

			t.Errorf("restore block %q: %v", key, err)
		}
	})
	if _, err := e.pool.Exec(ctx, `UPDATE homepage_blocks SET enabled = $2 WHERE key = $1`, key, enabled); err != nil {

## Claude · 2026-11-23T09:11:55.123Z

Backend committed. Now the Innert images: converting to WebP at the target widths, adjusting quality to stay at or under 300 KB per file.

**Tool: Write**

```
C:\Users\[USER]\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\27133936c4ab5668\60d9fad0-2c1e-4660-a781-65a98ec415b1\scratchpad\make_banners.py
---
# Converts the owner's Innert PNGs to WebP in several widths (<= 300 KB each). Originals are not copied.
import io
import sys

from PIL import Image

src_dir, out_dir = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
LIMIT = 300 * 1024
jobs = [
    ("hero-3840x1240.png", "innert-hero", [1280, 1920, 2560]),
    ("key-art-clean-1920x1080.png", "innert-mobile", [768, 1080]),
]
for src, base, widths in jobs:
    im = Image.open(f"{src_dir}/{src}").convert("RGB")
    for w in widths:
        h = round(im.height * w / im.width)
        small = im.resize((w, h), Image.LANCZOS)
        for q in (82, 78, 74, 70, 66, 62, 58):
            buf = io.BytesIO()
            small.save(buf, "WEBP", quality=q, method=6)
            if buf.tell() <= LIMIT:
                break
        name = f"{out_dir}/{base}-{w}.webp"
        open(name, "wb").write(buf.getvalue())
        print(f"{base}-{w}.webp {w}x{h} q={q} {buf.tell() // 1024} KB")
```

// Tiny helper for component/hook tests (jsdom). The project has no Testing Library, so we render
// with React's own createRoot inside act().
import { act, type ReactNode } from "react";
import { createRoot } from "react-dom/client";

// Tell React that this environment is a test (enables act() and silences its warning).
(globalThis as { IS_REACT_ACT_ENVIRONMENT?: boolean }).IS_REACT_ACT_ENVIRONMENT = true;

/** Renders `ui` into a fresh <div> that is attached to the document (so focus works). */
export function render(ui: ReactNode) {
  const container = document.createElement("div");
  document.body.append(container);
  const root = createRoot(container);
  act(() => root.render(ui));
  return {
    container,
    /** Renders something else into the same root (same component = its state is kept, props change). */
    rerender(next: ReactNode) {
      act(() => root.render(next));
    },
    unmount() {
      act(() => root.unmount());
      container.remove();
    },
  };
}

// Home page (Task 15, pkg/homepage/types.go). The page is a list of blocks from the server; each block
// has its own endpoint. The frontend has one renderer per block TYPE and no content of its own.
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

/**
 * One block of GET /api/homepage. `type` is a plain string on purpose: a newer server may send a type this

---
// Typed functions for the home page endpoints (Task 15). Every call goes through apiFetch.
import { apiFetch, type ApiOptions } from "./client.ts";
import type { HomepageResponse } from "./types.ts";

/** GET /api/homepage: which blocks the home page shows, in order, and where each one's content is. */
export function getHomepage(options?: ApiOptions): Promise<HomepageResponse> {
  return apiFetch<HomepageResponse>("/api/homepage", options);
}

/** Every block endpoint lives under this path (the server builds them in pkg/homepage/config.go). */
export const BLOCK_ENDPOINT_PREFIX = "/api/blocks/";

/**
 * GET one block's content from the endpoint the registry gave. The page only ever asks OUR block API:
 * an endpoint outside /api/blocks/ (which our server never sends) is refused instead of fetched,
 * and the block shows its error state.
 */
export function getBlock<T>(endpoint: string, options?: ApiOptions): Promise<T> {
  if (!endpoint.startsWith(BLOCK_ENDPOINT_PREFIX) || endpoint.includes("..")) {
    return Promise.reject(new Error(`Refusing to load a block from ${endpoint}`));
  }
  return apiFetch<T>(endpoint, options);
}
```

--- new
  /** Who is signed in (Task 14): the user or null. Sign in / out write this entry directly. */
  currentUser: ["auth", "me"] as const,
  /** The home page block registry (Task 15). */
  homepage: ["homepage"] as const,
  /** One home page block's content, keyed by its endpoint: every block is cached (and retried) on its own. */
  homeBlock: (endpoint: string) => ["homepage", "block", endpoint] as const,
};

/** Key + function of the home page registry (GET /api/homepage). */
export function homepageQuery() {
  return queryOptions({
    queryKey: queryKeys.homepage,
    queryFn: ({ signal }) => getHomepage({ signal }),
  });
}

/**
 * Key + function of one block. T is the block's JSON shape (HeroBlockData, ...): the renderer that asks for it
 * knows which type it draws. Each block is its own query, so one slow or failing block never holds up the others.
 */
export function homeBlockQuery<T>(endpoint: string) {
  return queryOptions({
    queryKey: queryKeys.homeBlock(endpoint),
    queryFn: ({ signal }) => getBlock<T>(endpoint, { signal }),
  });
}

---
import { useSyncExternalStore } from "react";

const QUERY = "(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)";

/** The media query list, or null where matchMedia does not exist (old browsers, some test environments). */
function mediaQuery(): MediaQueryList | null {
  return typeof window !== "undefined" && typeof window.matchMedia === "function" ? window.matchMedia(QUERY) : null;
}

function subscribe(onChange: () => void) {
  const mql = mediaQuery();
  mql?.addEventListener("change", onChange);
  return () => mql?.removeEventListener("change", onChange);
}

/**
 * True when the user asked the operating system to reduce motion. useSyncExternalStore keeps it LIVE:
 * switching the setting while the page is open updates every component that uses it.
 * CSS animations are already switched off globally (global.css); this is for motion started by JavaScript,
 * like the hero carousel's autoplay.
 */
export function usePrefersReducedMotion(): boolean {
  return useSyncExternalStore(subscribe, () => mediaQuery()?.matches ?? false);
}
```

import { useEffect, useState, type FocusEvent, type KeyboardEvent } from "react";
import { usePrefersReducedMotion } from "../../lib/usePrefersReducedMotion.ts";

/** How long a slide stays before the next one (owner decision: about 6 s). */
export const AUTOPLAY_MS = 6000;

/**
 * The state of the hero carousel (WAI-ARIA "carousel" pattern):
 *  - `index`: the slide on screen. next / prev wrap around; goTo jumps.
 *  - `playing`: the automatic slide show is ON (what the Play / Pause button shows). It starts ON, except when the
 *    user asked the system to reduce motion - then it starts OFF, and only the user can switch it on.
 *  - `rotating`: the slides are moving RIGHT NOW = playing, more than one slide, and the pointer is not over the
 *    carousel and keyboard focus is not inside it (nobody wants a slide to change while they read it or are about
 *    to click its button).
 * `rootProps` go on the carousel's outer element.
 */
export function useCarousel(count: number) {
  const reducedMotion = usePrefersReducedMotion();
  const [index, setIndex] = useState(0);
  // null = the user has not pressed Play / Pause yet, so the default (from reduced motion) applies.
  const [userPlaying, setUserPlaying] = useState<boolean | null>(null);
  const [hovered, setHovered] = useState(false);
  const [focused, setFocused] = useState(false);

  // A refetch may return fewer slides: never point past the end.
  const current = count > 0 ? Math.min(index, count - 1) : 0;
  const playing = count > 1 && (userPlaying ?? !reducedMotion);
  const rotating = playing && !hovered && !focused;

  // One timer per shown slide: any change of slide (automatic or by the user) starts a fresh 6 s.
  useEffect(() => {
    if (!rotating) return;
    const timer = setTimeout(() => setIndex((current + 1) % count), AUTOPLAY_MS);
    return () => clearTimeout(timer);
  }, [rotating, current, count]);

  const goTo = (i: number) => setIndex(((i % count) + count) % count);
  const next = () => goTo(current + 1);
  const prev = () => goTo(current - 1);

  return {
    index: current,
    playing,
    rotating,
    goTo,
    next,
    prev,
    togglePlaying: () => setUserPlaying(!playing),
    rootProps: {
      onMouseEnter: () => setHovered(true),
      onMouseLeave: () => setHovered(false),
      onFocus: () => setFocused(true),
      // Focus moving between two buttons INSIDE the carousel is not "leaving" it.
      
… [truncated; original 3048 chars]

---
/*
 * Hero carousel (DESIGN.md 3.4). Desktop / tablet: a fixed 288px tall box, the picture fills it (object-fit: cover)
 * and the text sits on the left over a dark scrim. Phones: a 16:9 box, the text at the bottom over a bottom scrim.
 * The box has its size BEFORE any picture loads, so nothing on the page moves (CLS).
 */
.carousel {
  position: relative;
  height: var(--hero-height);
  overflow: hidden;
  background: var(--color-surface-card);
}

.skeleton {
  width: 100%;
  height: 100%;
}

.errorFrame {
  display: grid;
  place-items: center;
  padding: var(--space-4);
}

/* All slides share one grid cell (stacked); only the active one is visible. A cross-fade, not a slide-in:
   the box never changes size and nothing outside it moves. */
.slides {
  display: grid;
  height: 100%;
}

.slide {
  position: relative;
  grid-area: 1 / 1;
  min-width: 0;
  opacity: 0;
  visibility: hidden;
  transition:
    opacity var(--slide-transition),
    visibility var(--slide-transition);
}

.active {
  opacity: 1;
  visibility: visible;
}

.picture,
.image {
  width: 100%;
  height: 100%;
}

.image {
  object-fit: cover;
}

/* The text column, over the scrim that keeps white text readable on light art. */
.content {
  position: absolute;
  inset: 0;
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  justify-content: center;
  align-items: flex-start;
  gap: var(--space-2);
  padding: var(--space-5) var(--space-7);
  background: var(--hero-scrim);
}

.content > * {
  max-width: var(--hero-content-max-width);
}

.badge {
  padding: var(--space-1) var(--space-2);
  border-radius: var(--radius-pill);
  background: var(--color-accent);

  font-size: var(--font-size-2xs);

.price {
  color: var(--color-text);
}

  title: string;

  retrying: boolean;
}

 * The small error of ONE home page block: a line of text and Retry. The rest of the page keeps working

      </Button>
    </div>
  );
}

import { Component, type ReactNode } from "react";

interface BlockBoundaryProps {
  /** For the console message: which block crashed. */
  blockKey: string;
  children: ReactNode;
}

/**
 * A React error boundary around ONE home page block. If the block's renderer throws while drawing (e.g. the
 * server sent data in a shape this build does not expect), only that block disappears; without it the error
 * would reach the route's error page and take the whole home page down.
 * (Error boundaries can only be class components - React has no hook for this.)
 */
export class BlockBoundary extends Component<BlockBoundaryProps, { failed: boolean }> {
  state = { failed: false };

  static getDerivedStateFromError() {
    return { failed: true };
  }

  componentDidCatch(error: unknown) {
    console.error(`Home page block "${this.props.blockKey}" failed to render and is hidden.`, error);
  }

  render() {
    return this.state.failed ? null : this.props.children;
  }
}
```

---
import type { ComponentType } from "react";
import { cx } from "../../lib/cx.ts";
import { BlockBoundary } from "./BlockBoundary.tsx";
import styles from "./BlockRenderer.module.css";
import type { BlockProps } from "./blockTypes.ts";
import { HeroCarousel } from "./HeroCarousel.tsx";
import { PlatformRow } from "./PlatformRow.tsx";
import { ProductRow } from "./ProductRow.tsx";
import { SideTiles } from "./SideTiles.tsx";

/**
 * ONE renderer per block TYPE. This map is everything the frontend knows about the home page: which blocks
 * exist, their order, titles and content all come from the server (GET /api/homepage + each block's endpoint).
 * A new kind of block = a new entry here (and an endpoint on the server).
 */
const RENDERERS: Record<string, ComponentType<BlockProps>> = {
  hero: HeroCarousel,
  tiles: SideTiles,
  platforms: PlatformRow,
  product_row: ProductRow,
};

/** CSS class of the slot a block type sits in (the hero and the tiles share a row on desktop). */
const SLOT_CLASS: Record<string, string | undefined> = {
  hero: styles.hero,
  tiles: styles.tiles,
};

/**
 * Draws one block of the registry. A type this build does not know (a newer server, an old open tab) is
 * skipped, not an error. Each block is wrapped in its own error boundary: a block that crashes hides itself.
 */
export function BlockRenderer({ block }: BlockProps) {
  // Object.hasOwn: a type like "toString" must not find Object.prototype.toString.
  const Renderer = Object.hasOwn(RENDERERS, block.type) ? RENDERERS[block.type] : undefined;
  if (!Renderer) return null;
  return (
    <div className={cx(styles.slot, SLOT_CLASS[block.type])} data-block-type={block.type}>
      <BlockBoundary blockKey={block.key}>
        <Renderer block={block} />
      </BlockBoundary>
    </div>
  );
}
```

---
/*
 * Block slots on the home page grid (HomePage.module.css: two columns, "1fr auto"). Every block takes the
 * full width - except that on desktop the hero and the tiles that FOLLOW it share one row, like Eneba's banner
 * row: the carousel on the left, the tiles on the right. If either is switched off, the other is simply full
 * width; the page needs no code for that.
 */
.slot {
  grid-column: 1 / -1;
  min-width: 0;
}

.hero:has(+ .tiles) {
  grid-column: 1;
}

/* Next to the carousel the tiles are narrow and as tall as the hero (SideTiles reads these two variables). */
.hero + .tiles {
  grid-column: 2;
  --tile-width: var(--hero-tile-width);
  --tile-height: var(--hero-height);
}

/* Tablets and phones: the tiles go under the carousel, full width (the variables fall back to their defaults). */
@media (max-width: 930px) {
  .hero:has(+ .tiles),
  .hero + .tiles {
    grid-column: 1 / -1;
  }

  .hero + .tiles {
    --tile-width: initial;
    --tile-height: initial;
  }
}

import { useQuery } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { Link } from "react-router";

const ACCENTS: Record<string, string | undefined> = {
  violet: styles.violet,
  promo: styles.promo,
  mint: styles.mint,
};

/** Placeholders while the tiles load (the usual number of tiles, so the row keeps its size). */

---
/*
 * Platform row (DESIGN.md 3.4), after Eneba's category row: 100px wide entries, a 32px icon above a 12px bold
 * label, 5px apart, starting 40px under the hero. Our entries also show the product count. Scrolls sideways
 * when it does not fit.
 */
.nav {
  max-width: var(--page-max-width);
  margin: 0 auto;
  padding: var(--space-6) var(--page-gutter) 0;
}

.row {
  display: flex;
  gap: var(--space-1);
  overflow-x: auto;
}

.row > li {
  flex-shrink: 0;
}

.item {
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  align-items: center;
  gap: var(--space-1);
  width: var(--platform-item-width);
  padding: var(--space-2) 0;
  color: var(--color-text);
  text-align: center;
  transition: color var(--transition-fast);
}

.item:hover {
  color: var(--color-accent);
}

.icon {
  width: var(--platform-icon-size);
  height: var(--platform-icon-size);
  margin-bottom: var(--space-1);
}

.label {
  font-size: var(--font-size-xs);
  font-weight: var(--font-weight-bold);
  line-height: var(--line-height-snug);
}

.count {
  color: var(--color-text-muted);
  font-size: var(--font-size-xs);
  line-height: var(--line-height-snug);
}

.item:hover .count {
  color: inherit;
}

.skeletonIcon {
  width: var(--platform-icon-size);
  height: var(--platform-icon-size);
}

.skeletonLabel {
  width: 70%;
  height: var(--font-size-xs);
  margin-top: var(--space-2);
}

@media (max-width: 600px) {
  .nav {
    padding-top: var(--space-4);
  }
}

.skeleton {
  display: block;
  background: linear-gradient(
    90deg,
    var(--color-skeleton) 0%,
    var(--color-skeleton-shine) 50%,
    var(--color-skeleton) 100%
  );
  background-size: 200% 100%;
  border-radius: var(--radius-none);

  width: 100%;
  height: 1em;

  height: 100%;

    background-position: -200% 0;

import type { ReactNode } from "react";
import styles from "./EmptyState.module.css";

interface EmptyStateProps {
  title: string;
  /** Heading level of the title: h2 inside a page (default), h1 when this block IS the whole page (404). */
  as?: "h1" | "h2";
  /** One line of help text. */
  message?: string;
  /** Optional action under the text: a Button ("Reset filters") or a Link. */
  children?: ReactNode;
}

/** "Nothing here" block (no results, page not found...). Centered inside the content area (DESIGN.md 4.9). */
export function EmptyState({ title, as: Heading = "h2", message, children }: EmptyStateProps) {
  return (
    <div className={styles.state}>
      <Heading className={styles.title}>{title}</Heading>
      {message && <p className={styles.message}>{message}</p>}
      {children}
    </div>
  );
}

c:\PROJEKTAI\ENEBA\ENEBA-homework\src\features\home\ProductRow.module.css
---
/*
 * Product row (DESIGN.md 3.4), measured on Eneba's home page: a full-bleed band (every second row darker,
 * BlockRenderer.module.css), a 30px bold heading 30px above five cards, and a centred 194 x 35 outlined
 * "View all" 40px under them. Tablets: the cards scroll sideways; phones: the Store's list cards, one per line.
 */
.band {
  padding: var(--space-6) 0;
}

.inner {
  max-width: var(--page-max-width);
  margin: 0 auto;
  padding: 0 var(--page-gutter);
}

.title {
  margin-bottom: var(--space-5);
  font-size: var(--font-size-4xl);
  line-height: var(--line-height-tight);
}

.grid {
  display: grid;
  grid-template-columns: repeat(5, minmax(0, 1fr));
  gap: var(--grid-gap);
}

.item {
  min-width: 0;
}

.viewAll {
  display: flex;
  justify-content: center;
  margin-top: var(--space-6);
}

.viewAllButton {
  width: var(--view-all-width);
}

/* Tablets: one line of cards that scrolls sideways (snapping to a card), instead of squeezing five in. */
@media (max-width: 930px) {
  .grid {
    grid-template-columns: none;
    grid-auto-columns: var(--row-card-width);
    grid-auto-flow: column;
    overflow-x: auto;
    scroll-snap-type: x mandatory;
    padding-bottom: var(--space-2); /* room for the scrollbar */
  }

  .item {
    scroll-snap-align: start;
  }
}

/* Phones: ProductCard turns into a horizontal list card by itself; the row becomes a plain column. */
@media (max-width: 600px) {
  .band {
    padding: var(--space-5) 0;
  }

/**
 * The home page (Task 15) is assembled from blocks the SERVER lists (GET /api/homepage): which blocks, in which

 * breaks the others. There is no home page content in the frontend.
 *

 * product row bands run edge to edge, like Eneba's; each block centres its own content.

export function HomePage() {
  useDocumentTitle(); // home = no page name in the tab title

      <h1>

      </h1>

        // footer is not on screen and nothing visible moves when the blocks come (CLS).
        <div className={styles.loading} aria-busy="true">
          <Skeleton variant="block" className={styles.heroSkeleton} />
        </div>
      )}

      {query.isError && (
        <div className={styles.inner}>
          <ErrorState message="The home page could not be loaded." onRetry={() => void query.refetch()} retrying={query.isFetching} />
        </div>
      )}

      {query.data && query.data.blocks.length === 0 && (
        <div className={styles.inner}>
          <EmptyState title="Nothing to show here yet" message="The store is open, though.">
            <But

---
/*
 * Home page (DESIGN.md 3.4). The page is full-bleed (the layout drops its max width for it); the blocks sit on a
 * two-column grid so the hero and the tiles can share the first row on desktop (BlockRenderer.module.css).
 */
.page {
  width: 100%;
}

.blocks {
  display: grid;
  grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr) auto;
}

/* Centred content for the page-level states (error, empty). */
.inner {
  max-width: var(--page-max-width);
  margin: 0 auto;
  padding: var(--space-4) var(--page-gutter);
}

.loading {
  min-height: 100vh;
}

.heroSkeleton {
  height: var(--hero-height);
}

@media (max-width: 600px) {
  .heroSkeleton {
    height: auto;
    aspect-ratio: var(--hero-aspect-ratio-sm);
  }
}

   directly under the header). It marks itself with data-full-bleed-page and centres each block's content itself. */

  max-width: none;
  padding: 0;
}

// Screenshots of OUR home page (vite preview on 127.0.0.1:4195), desktop + phone. Usage: node shots.mjs <outDir> [path]
import puppeteer from "puppeteer-core";
const out = process.argv[2];
const path = process.argv[3] ?? "/";
const browser = await puppeteer.launch({
  executablePath: "C:/Program Files (x86)/Microsoft/Edge/Application/msedge.exe",
  headless: "new",
});
for (const [w, h, name] of [[1440, 900, "desktop"], [390, 844, "mobile"], [768, 1024, "tablet"]]) {
  const page = await browser.newPage();
  const errors = [];
  page.on("console", (m) => m.type() === "error" && errors.push(m.text()));
  page.on("pageerror", (e) => errors.push(String(e)));
  await page.setViewport({ width: w, height: h, isMobile: w < 600, hasTouch: w < 600 });
  await page.goto("http://127.0.0.1:4195" + path, { waitUntil: "networkidle0", timeout: 60000 });
  await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 800));
  await page.screenshot({ path: `${out}/ours-${name}-top.png` });
  await page.screenshot({ path: `${out}/ours-${name}-full.png`, fullPage: true });
  const info = await page.evaluate(() => ({
    scrollW: document.documentElement.scrollWidth,
    clientW: document.documentElement.clientWidth,
    h: document.documentElement.scrollHeight,
  }));
  console.log(name, JSON.stringify(info), errors.length ? "ERRORS: " + errors.join(" | ") : "no console errors");
  await page.close();
}
await browser.close();
```

// Rectangles + a few computed styles of selectors on our page. Usage: node inspect.mjs <width> <height> <selector>...
import puppeteer from "puppeteer-core";
const [w, h, ...selectors] = process.argv.slice(2);
const browser = await puppeteer.launch({
  executablePath: "C:/Program Files (x86)/Microsoft/Edge/Application/msedge.exe",
  headless: "new",
});
const page = await browser.newPage();
await page.setViewport({ width: Number(w), height: Number(h), isMobile: Number(w) < 600, hasTouch: Number(w) < 600 });
await page.goto("http://127.0.0.1:4195/", { waitUntil: "networkidle0", timeout: 60000 });
const res = await page.evaluate((sels) => {
  return sels.flatMap((sel) =>
    [...document.querySelectorAll(sel)].slice(0, 4).map((el) => {
      const b = el.getBoundingClientRect();
      const cs = getComputedStyle(el);
      return `${sel} [${Math.round(b.x)},${Math.round(b.y)} ${Math.round(b.width)}x${Math.round(b.height)}] pos=${cs.position} disp=${cs.display} op=${cs.opacity} vis=${cs.visibility} color=${cs.color} bg=${cs.backgroundImage.slice(0, 40)} fs=${cs.fontSize} cur=${el.currentSrc ?? ""}`;
    }),
  );
}, selectors);
console.log(res.join("\n"));
await browser.close();
```

## Claude · 2026-11-23T09:22:05.315Z

The `.slides` grid row is auto-sized, so it grows to the tallest picture (the 16:10 Witcher art gives 631 px) and the 288 px box clips it. Fixing the track to `minmax(0, 1fr)`:

**Tool: Edit**

```
c:\PROJEKTAI\ENEBA\ENEBA-homework\src\features\home\HeroCarousel.module.css
--- old
.slides {
  display: grid;
  height: 100%;
}

.slide {
  position: relative;
  grid-area: 1 / 1;
  min-width: 0;
--- new
.slides {
  display: grid;
  /* One cell exactly the size of the box: an "auto" row would grow to the tallest picture and be cut off. */
  grid-template: minmax(0, 1fr) / minmax(0, 1fr);
  height: 100%;
}

.slide {
  position: relative;
  grid-area: 1 / 1;
  min-width: 0;
  min-height: 0;
```

---
// @vitest-environment jsdom
// The home page in an in-memory router, with apiFetch replaced by a fake server (no network). It checks that the
// page is built ONLY from what the server sends, and that the blocks fail independently.
import { QueryClient, QueryClientProvider } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { act } from "react";
import { createMemoryRouter, RouterProvider } from "react-router";
import { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import { ApiError, apiFetch } from "../api/client.ts";
import { discounts, homeResponses, registry, tiles } from "../features/home/fixtures.ts";
import { render } from "../test-utils.tsx";
import { HomePage } from "./HomePage.tsx";

vi.mock("../api/client.ts", async (importOriginal) => ({
  ...(await importOriginal<typeof import("../api/client.ts")>()),
  apiFetch: vi.fn(),
}));

const fakeApi = vi.mocked(apiFetch);
const requested = () => fakeApi.mock.calls.map((call) => call[0]);
/** What the fake server answers, per path. A missing path or an Error value = HTTP 500. */
let responses: Record<string, unknown>;

beforeEach(() => {
  responses = { ...homeResponses };
  fakeApi.mockReset();
  fakeApi.mockImplementation(async (path: string) => {
    const answer = responses[path];
    if (answer === undefined || answer instanceof Error) throw new ApiError("http", "HTTP 500", 500);
    return structuredClone(answer) as never;
  });
});

let cleanup: (() => void) | undefined;
afterEach(() => {
  cleanup?.();
  vi.restoreAllMocks();
});

const settle = async () => {
  for (let tick = 0; tick < 6; tick++) await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 0));
};
const flush = () => act(settle);

async function mount() {
  const router = createMemoryRouter(
    [
      { path: "/", Component: HomePage },
      { path: "/store", element: <p>store page</p> },
      { path: "/product/:slug", element: <p>product page</p> },
    ],
    { initialEntries: ["/"] },
  );
  const queryClient = new QueryClient({ defaultOptions: { queries: { retry: false } } });
  const view = render(
    <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>
      <RouterProvider router={router} />
    </QueryClientProvider>,
  );
  cleanup = view.unmount;
  await flush();
  return { router };
}

---
// @vitest-environment jsdom
// The hero carousel: slides, LCP picture, autoplay (6 s), pause on hover / focus, reduced motion, keyboard.
// The block's data is put into the query cache up front, so no request is made; timers are fake.
import { QueryClient, QueryClientProvider } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { act } from "react";
import { createMemoryRouter, RouterProvider } from "react-router";
import { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import { apiFetch } from "../../api/client.ts";
import { queryKeys } from "../../api/queries.ts";
import type { HeroBlockData } from "../../api/types.ts";
import { render } from "../../test-utils.tsx";
import { hero } from "./fixtures.ts";
import { HeroCarousel } from "./HeroCarousel.tsx";
import { AUTOPLAY_MS } from "./useCarousel.ts";

vi.mock("../../api/client.ts", async (importOriginal) => ({
  ...(await importOriginal<typeof import("../../api/client.ts")>()),
  apiFetch: vi.fn(),
}));

const block = { key: "hero", type: "hero", title: "Featured", endpoint: "/api/blocks/hero" };

/** A fake matchMedia for "(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)"; `set` flips it like the OS setting would. */
function fakeReducedMotion(matches: boolean) {
  const listeners = new Set<() => void>();
  const mql = {
    matches,
    media: "(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)",
    addEventListener: (_: string, l: () => void) => listeners.add(l),
    removeEventListener: (_: string, l: () => void) => listeners.delete(l),
  };
  window.matchMedia = vi.fn(() => mql) as never;
  return {
    set(value: boolean) {
      mql.matches = value;
      act(() => listeners.forEach((l) => l()));
    },
  };
}

let cleanup: (() => void) | undefined;
beforeEach(() => {
  vi.useFakeTimers();
  vi.mocked(apiFetch).mockReset();
  fakeReducedMotion(false);
});
afterEach(() => {
  cleanup?.();
  vi.useRealTimers();
});

function mount(data: HeroBlockData = hero) {
  const queryClient = new QueryClient({ defaultOptions: { queries: { retry: false, staleTime: Infinity } } });
  queryClient.setQueryData(queryKeys.homeBlock(block.endpoint), data);
  const router = createMemoryRouter([{ path: "/", element: <HeroCarousel block={block} /> }]);
  const view = render(
    <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>
      <RouterProvider router={router} />
    </QueryClientProvider>,
  );
  cleanup = view.unmount;
}

import { apiFetch } from "./client.ts";
import { getBlock, getHomepage } from "./homepage.ts";

  apiFetch: vi.fn(async () => ({ ok: true })),
}));

  });

    "api/blocks/hero",
    "",
  ])("getBlock refuses %j (only our block API is ever asked)", async (endpoint) => {
    await expect(getBlock(endpoint)).rejects.toThrow("Refusing to load a block");

  });
});

// Usage: node toggle.mjs <envFile> <key> <true|false>
import fs from "node:fs";
import pg from "pg";
const [envFile, key, value] = process.argv.slice(2);
const env = Object.fromEntries(
  fs.readFileSync(envFile, "utf8").split(/\r?\n/).filter((l) => l.includes("=") && !l.startsWith("#"))
    .map((l) => [l.slice(0, l.indexOf("=")).trim(), l.slice(l.indexOf("=") + 1).trim().replace(/^["']|["']$/g, "")]),
);
const client = new pg.Client({ connectionString: env.DATABASE_URL_DIRECT });
await client.connect();
const res = await client.query("UPDATE homepage_blocks SET enabled = $2 WHERE key = $1 RETURNING key, enabled", [key, value === "true"]);
console.log(JSON.stringify(res.rows));
const all = await client.query("SELECT key, enabled FROM homepage_blocks ORDER BY position, id");
console.log(all.rows.map((r) => `${r.key}=${r.enabled}`).join(" "));
await client.end();
EOF
echo ok
```

---
# Contrast of each text colour against every background pixel behind it (WCAG relative luminance).
import json
import re
import sys

from PIL import Image


def lin(c):
    c /= 255
    return c / 12.92 if c <= 0.03928 else ((c + 0.055) / 1.055) ** 2.4


def lum(r, g, b):
    return 0.2126 * lin(r) + 0.7152 * lin(g) + 0.0722 * lin(b)


meta = json.load(open(sys.argv[1], encoding="utf-8"))
for m in meta:
    nums = [float(v) for v in re.findall(r"[\d.]+", m["color"])]
    r, g, b = nums[:3]
    a = nums[3] if len(nums) > 3 else 1.0
    im = Image.open(m["file"]).convert("RGB")
    ratios = []
    for (pr, pg, pb) in im.getdata():
        # the text colour composited over this pixel (muted text is semi-transparent white)
        tr, tg, tb = (a * r + (1 - a) * pr, a * g + (1 - a) * pg, a * b + (1 - a) * pb)
        l1, l2 = lum(tr, tg, tb), lum(pr, pg, pb)
        hi, lo = max(l1, l2), min(l1, l2)
        ratios.append((hi + 0.05) / (lo + 0.05))
    ratios.sort()
    large = m["fs"] >= 24 or (m["fs"] >= 18.66 and m["fw"] >= 700)
    need = 3.0 if large else 4.5
    p5 = ratios[len(ratios) // 20]
    flag = "OK " if ratios[0] >= need else ("ok*" if p5 >= need else "LOW")
    text = m["text"].encode("ascii", "replace").decode()
    print(f"{flag} {m['name']:7} slide{m['slide']} {text[:24]:24} {m['fs']:.0f}px need {need}: min {ratios[0]:.2f}  p5 {p5:.2f}")
```

# Prints the parts of a Lighthouse JSON report this task needs: scores, LCP, CLS, the LCP element and its loading.
import json
import sys

r = json.load(open(sys.argv[1], encoding="utf-8"))
a = r["audits"]
cats = {k: round(v["score"] * 100) for k, v in r["categories"].items()}
print("scores", cats)
for k in ["largest-contentful-paint", "cumulative-layout-shift", "first-contentful-paint", "total-blocking-time"]:
    print(f"{k}: {a[k]['displayValue']}")
lcp = a.get("largest-contentful-paint-element", {})
for item in lcp.get("details", {}).get("items", []):
    for sub in item.get("items", []):
        node = sub.get("node")
        if node:
            print("LCP element:", node.get("snippet", "")[:220])
for k in ["lcp-lazy-loaded", "prioritize-lcp-image", "layout-shifts", "unsized-images"]:
    if k in a:
        print(f"{k}: score={a[k]['score']} {a[k].get('displayValue', '')}")
```

# Data model

The whole schema of the marketplace, designed once. The **catalog** tables exist
(migration `00002_catalog.sql`, plus `game_aliases` for search from `00003_search_aliases.sql`), and so do the **auth** tables
(`00004_auth.sql`, Task 14). The **commerce** tables (`00005_orders.sql`, Task 16a) are built as

| Group | Tables | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Catalog | `platforms`, `regions`, `games`, `products`, `sellers`, `offers`, `keys` | **created** – migration `00002` |
| Search | `game_aliases` | **created** – migration `00003` (Task 13a) |
| Auth | `users`, `sessions`, `login_failures` | **created** – migration `00004` (Task 14) |
| Commerce | `orders`, `order_items`, `payments` + column `keys.order_item_id` | **created** – migration `00005` (Task 16a) |

Ground rules used everywhere:
- **Money is integer cents, EUR only** (`price_cents integer`). Never `float`/`numeric` for money.
- **IDs:** `bigint GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY` (the database owns the numbers; a manual `INSERT` of an id fails, so seeds refer to rows by slug). Exception: `orders.id` is a **UUID** – it appears in URLs and must not reveal how many orders exist.
- **Slugs** (`the-witcher-3-pc-global`) are the public, URL-friendly identifiers; numeric ids stay internal. Every slug column has the same CHECK `^[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*$` (lower

section = r"""## Home page tables (created – migration `00006`, Task 15)

The home page is assembled from **blocks**. The registry (`homepage_blocks`) says which blocks the page shows, in which
order and with which titles; `GET /api/homepage` returns the enabled rows. Every block has its **own endpoint and data
source** (`pkg/homepage`): the hero reads `homepage_slides` (+ live prices from the catalog), the tiles read `homepage_tiles`
(+ cover images from the catalog), the platform row and the product rows read only the catalog. The frontend has one
renderer per block **type** and no content of its own.

**The content rows are inserted by the migration, not by `cmd/seed`** (owner decision): the catalog seed and its golden
checksum stay unchanged, and production needs no reseed (`seed --reset` refuses once orders exist). `seed --reset` only
deletes the catalog tables, so it never touches these.

**No foreign key into the catalog:** `homepage_slides.product_slug` is plain text. The blocks are logically separate
services (each could move to its own service and database), and the catalog reseed deletes and re-inserts products, which
an FK would block. The API checks instead: a slide whose product does not exist or is out of stock is skipped.

Backward compatible (expand): three new tables; the code that is live while the migration runs never reads them.

### `homepage_blocks` (the registry)
| Column | Type | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | `bigint` identity, PK | tie-breaker of the order |
| `key` | `text` NN UNIQUE, slug CHECK | stable name; a product row's endpoint

## Claude · 2026-11-23T09:36:50.592Z

I'll compute the contrast numbers for the new band colour before writing them down:

**Tool: Bash**

```
$ python -I -c "
def lin(c):
    c/=255; return c/12.92 if c<=0.03928 else ((c+0.055)/1.055)**2.4
def L(h): r,g,b=(int(h[i:i+2],16) for i in (1,3,5)); return .2126*lin(r)+.7152*lin(g)+.0722*lin(b)
def mix(fg,a,bg):
    f=[int(fg[i:i+2],16) for i in (1,3,5)]; b=[int(bg[i:i+2],16) for i in (1,3,5)]
    return '#'+''.join('%02x'%round(a*x+(1-a)*y) for x,y in zip(f,b))
def cr(a,b): x,y=sorted([L(a),L(b)]); return round((y+.05)/(x+.05),2)
band='#311178'
print('white/band', cr('#ffffff',band), 'muted/band', cr(mix('#ffffff',.7,band),band), 'accent/band', cr('#fad318',band), 'secondary/band', cr('#23c299',band))
print('white border vs band (view all)', cr('#ffffff',band), 'band vs page', cr(band,'#4618ac'))
"
```

> **Disclaimer & Attribution:** This repository is a non-commercial portfolio and internship assignment project demonstrating marketplace UI and architecture. It is not affiliated with, endorsed by, or connected to Eneba (JSC "Helis play"). Authentication and checkout are strictly simulated – no real payments, user credentials, or digital keys are processed or issued. Game metadata and cover images are provided by IGDB under the Twitch Developer Service Agreement for non-commercial use. All product names, logos, brands and artwork remain the property of their respective owners.

# EneHomeworkBa (ENEBA homework)

## Stack
- Frontend: React + TypeScript (Vite), repo root
- Backend: Go (`net/http` ServeMux) as a single Vercel serverless function
- Database: Neon Postgres via `pgx` v5 / `pgxpool`, **pooled** connection (`-pooler` host)
- Hosting: Vercel, region `fra1` (Frankfurt)

## Run locally
Requirements: Go 1.25+ (see `go.mod`), Node 20+.
## API
JSON, `camelCase` field names. Money is **integer cents** (formatting is the frontend's job). Errors are always
`{"error":"..."}`; an unreachable database gives `503` and any other unexpected error `500`, both with a generic message (never SQL or driver text).

| Endpoint | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `GET /api/health` | API + DB status (see above) |
| `GET /api/products` | Store listing: `items`, `total`, `page`, `pageSize` and `facets` (filter options with counts) |
| `GET /api/products/{slug}` | Product page: `product`, `game`, `offers`
… [truncated; original 19982 chars]
```
</details>

The assignment: *the landing page is assembled from several different blocks; each block gets its content from its own
backend service or database, so the frontend only displays what it receives and nothing is hardcoded there.*

- **Block registry:** the table `homepage_blocks` lists the blocks (`key`, `type`, `title`, `position`, `enabled`, `config`).
  `GET /api/homepage` returns the enabled ones in order: `{"blocks":[{"key","type","title","endpoint"}]}`. The frontend
  (`src/features/home/`) has **one renderer per type** (`hero`, `tiles`, `platforms`, `product_row`) and fetches every block from its
  own `endpoint` with its own TanStack Query; it contains no block content, order or titles. An unknown type is skipped.
- **Each block is its own service** (`pkg/homepage`; handlers in `pkg/server/homepage_handlers.go`):

| Endpoint | Data source | Answers |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/homepage` | `homepage_blocks` | the registry (above) |
| `GET /api/blocks/hero` | `homepage_slides` + the catalog (live cheapest price of a product slide) | `{"slides":[{id,title,subtitle,badge,image:{url,srcset,mobileUrl,mobileSrcset},cta:{label,href}\\|null,priceCents\\|null}]}` |
| `GET /api/blocks/tiles` | `homepage_tiles` + the catalog (up to 3 cover images per tile) | `{"tiles":[{id,title,subtitle,href,accent,coverUrls}]}` |
| `GET /api/blocks/platforms` | the catalog (the Store's platform facet) + the block's icon config | `{"items":[{key,label,count,href,icon}]}` ("All games" first) |
| `GET /api/blocks/products/{key}` | the Store listing query, configured by the block row (`sort`, `limit`, `viewAllHref`) | `{"items":[Item],"viewAllHref"}` |

  Today they share one Go function and one database, but they only meet in the registry: each could move to its own
  service (a CMS for the hero and tiles, the catalog service for the rest) and only the endpoint URLs in Go would change.
- **Content lives in the database, inserted by migration `00006`** (not by the seed, so the catalog checksum is unchanged and
  production needs no reseed). Switching a block off: `UPDATE homepage_b
… [truncated; original 5111 chars]
```

# Task 15 – pagrindinis puslapis iš backend blokų

## Blokų registras ir kodėl frontend'e tik „piešėjai" (`homepage_blocks`, `GET /api/homepage`, `src/features/home/BlockRenderer.tsx`)
- **Ką daro:** lentelė `homepage_blocks` sako, kokie blokai yra puslapyje, kokia tvarka (`position`), kokiais pavadinimais ir ar įjungti (`enabled`). `GET /api/homepage` grąžina įjungtus: `{ blocks: [{ key, type, title, endpoint }] }`. Frontend'e yra **vienas komponentas kiekvienam tipui** (`RENDERERS`: `hero` → `HeroCarousel`, `tiles` → `SideTiles`, `platforms` → `PlatformRow`, `product_row` → `ProductRow`); kiekvienas pats atsisiunčia savo turinį iš savo `endpoint` (atskira TanStack užklausa, raktas `["homepage", "block", endpoint]`).
- **Kodėl taip:** užduotis sako „frontend'as tik rodo, ką gauna, niekas nehardkodinta". Tvarką, pavadinimus, skaidres, plyteles ir produktų eilutes keičia duomenų bazė – **be deploy'o**. Nežinomas tipas (pvz., naujesnis serveris, o naršyklėje dar senas JS) tiesiog praleidžiamas, ne klaida.
- **`endpoint` nesaugomas DB:** Go jį **sukuria** iš tipo (`homepage.Endpoint`: `/api/blocks/hero`, `/api/blocks/products/{key}` …). Taip DB eilutė niekada negali nukreipti puslapio į svetimą adresą; papildomai `getBlock` (`src/api/homepage.ts`) atsisako bet ko, kas neprasideda `/api/blocks/`.
- **Kiekvienas blokas – atskiras „servisas":** herojus skaito `homepage_slides` + katalogą (gyva kaina), plytelės – `homepage_tiles` + katalogą (viršeliai), platformos ir produktų eilutės – tik katalogą (tą pačią Store užklausą `ListProducts`). Jie susitinka tik registre, todėl kiekvieną galima iškelti į atskirą servisą (pvz., CMS herojui), keičiant tik endpoint'ų adresus Go kode.
- **Konfigūracija griežta (`pkg/homepage/config.go`):** `config` JSON dekoduojamas su `DisallowUnknownFields`; nežinomas laukas, neleistinas `sort`, `limit` ne 1–12, išorinė nuoroda → **klaida (500)**, ne tylus numatytasis. Rašybos klaida konfigūracijoje turi būti pastebėta, o ne rodyti „kažkokius" produktus.
- **Gali paklausti:** „O jei reikės naujo bloko tipo?" – reikia kodo abiejose pusėse (endpoint + renderer) ir migracijos (CHECK leidžia tik žinomus tipus). Turinys ir tvarka – tik DB. „Kodėl hero ir tiles toje pačioje eilutėje, jei tai du blokai?" – CSS: `.hero:has(+ .tiles)` – jei plytelės eina tiesiai po herojaus, jos dalijasi eilute; išjungus vieną, kitas tiesiog per visą plotį.

… [truncated; original 8322 chars]
